\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\asymp\rho^{t}
, the above proposition shows that both signal and cross terms behave similar to their expected counterparts in Proposition
3
, and their deviation diminishes exponentially fast.

Proposition 9
.

Consider the iterations of SubGM on
f_{\ell_{1}}(U)
with the step-size defined as (
25
). Suppose that the measurements satisfy
(4r,\varepsilon,\delta,\mathcal{S})
-Sign-RIP with
\delta\lesssim 1/\sqrt{r}
,
\varepsilon=\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}
, and
\mathcal{S}=\left\{X:\zeta\leq\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}\leq R\right\}
for
\zeta=\varepsilon\left(1/\delta\vee\sqrt{d}\right)
and
R=5\sqrt{r}\sigma_{1}
. Moreover, suppose that
S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\succ 0
,
\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq\sigma_{1}
,
\left\lVert S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq 1.1\sigma_{1}
,
\left\lVert E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\right\rVert\leq 1/3
, and
\Delta_{t}\in\mathcal{S}
is
\varepsilon
-approximate rank-
4r
. Then, the following statements hold:

•

If
\eta\lesssim\frac{1}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert G_{t+1}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\!\left(1\!+\!\frac{\eta^{2}\rho^{2t}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert^{2}}\left(2\left\lVert E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}\!+\!\left\lVert E_{t}\right\rVert^{4}\!+\!2\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\left\lVert E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\right)\!+\!49\sqrt{r}\eta_{0}\rho^{t}\delta\right)\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert,

(29)

which can be further simplified as

\displaystyle\left\lVert G_{t+1}\right\rVert\leq\left(1+5\eta^{2}\rho^{2t}+49\sqrt{r}\eta_{0}\rho^{t}\delta\right)\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert.

(30)

•

If
\frac{\eta\rho^{t}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sigma_{1}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert F_{t+1}\right\rVert\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\eta\rho^{t}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)+16\sqrt{r}\eta\rho^{t}\delta\right)\left\lVert F_{t}\right\rVert+16\sqrt{r}\eta\rho^{t}\delta\left\lVert S_{t}\right\rVert+6\eta\rho^{t}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert.

(31)

The proof of Proposition
9
follows that of Proposition
6
, and can be found in Appendix
E.4
.
Inequality (
30
) implies that the growth rate of
\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert
diminishes with
t
. We will use this property to show that
\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert
remains proportional to the initialization scale
\alpha
throughout the solution trajectory, which will be used to control the final generalization error.
Moreover, unlike the dynamic of
\left\lVert F_{t}\right\rVert
, (
30
) holds even when
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert
decays faster than
\eta\sigma_{1}\rho^{t}
; this will play a key role in the proof of our next theorem.

Theorem 8

(Global Convergence of SubGM with Noisy Measurements)
.

Consider the iterations of SubGM on
f_{\ell_{1}}(U)
with the step-size defined as (
25
), and parameters
\eta\lesssim{1}/\left({\kappa\log(1/\alpha)}\right)
and
\rho=1-\Theta\left({\eta}/({\kappa\log(1/\alpha)})\right)
.
Suppose that the initial point
U_{0}
is obtained from Algorithm
2
with an initialization scale that satisfies
\alpha\lesssim{1}/({\sqrt{d}})\wedge{1}/{\kappa}\wedge\underline{\varphi}
. Suppose that the measurements satisfy
\left(4r,\delta,\varepsilon,\mathcal{S}\right)
-Sign-RIP with
\delta\lesssim{1}/\left({\sqrt{r}\kappa^{2}\bar{\varphi}^{4}\log^{2}\left(1/\alpha\right)}\right)
,
\varepsilon\asymp\sqrt{d}\alpha^{2-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{r}\kappa\delta\right)}\delta
, and
\mathcal{S}=\left\{X:\zeta\leq\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}\leq R\right\}
for
\zeta\geq\varepsilon\left(1/\delta\vee\sqrt{d}\right)
and
R=5\sqrt{r}\sigma_{1}
.
Then, after
T_{end}\lesssim{(\kappa/\eta)\log^{2}\left(1/{\alpha}\right)}
iterations, we have

\left\lVert U_{t}U_{t}^{\top}-X^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\lesssim d\alpha^{2-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{r}\kappa\delta\right)}\vee\zeta.

The proof of the above theorem can be found in Section
A.3
. Upon defining
\beta(\delta)=2-\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{r}\kappa\delta)
, the above result implies that, for any arbitrary accuracy
\varepsilon\geq\zeta
, SubGM converges to a solution that satisfies
\left\lVert U_{t}U_{t}^{\top}-X^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\varepsilon
within
\mathcal{O}(\kappa\log^{2}(d/\varepsilon)/(\beta(\delta)\eta))
iterations, provided that
\alpha\lesssim(\varepsilon/d)^{1/\beta(\delta)}
. Compared to the noiseless setting, the final error in Theorem (
8
) has an additional term
\zeta
. This is due to the fact that we only require a lower bound on the choice of
\zeta
; as will be explained later, this additional freedom will be used to show the convergence of SubGM under the Gaussian noise model. Moreover, compared to the noiseless setting, the iteration complexity of SubGM in the noisy regime is higher by a factor of
\log(d/\varepsilon)
, and its step-size must be chosen more conservatively. The higher iteration complexity is due to the lack of a prior estimate of
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}
; to alleviate this issue, we proposed a geometric step-size, which inevitably lead to a slightly higher iteration complexity.

Equipped with the above theorem and Theorems
4
and
5
, we next characterize the behavior of SubGM under both outlier and Gaussian noise regimes.

Corollary 2

(Outlier Noise Model)
.

Suppose that the measurement matrices
\left\{A_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{m}
have i.i.d. standard Gaussian entries, and the noise vector
\mathbf{s}
follows an outlier noise model with a corruption probability
0\leq p<1
(Definition
4
). Consider the iterations of SubGM on
{f}_{\ell_{1}}(U)
with the step-size defined as (
11
), and parameters
\eta\lesssim{1}/\left({\kappa\log(1/\alpha)}\right)
and
\rho=1-\Theta\left({\eta}/({\kappa\log(1/\alpha)})\right)
. Suppose that the initial point
U_{0}
is obtained from Algorithm
2
with an initialization scale that satisfies
\alpha\lesssim 1/\sqrt{d}\wedge{1}/{\kappa}\wedge(1-p)
. Suppose that the number of measurements satisfies
m\gtrsim\kappa^{4}dr^{2}\log^{5}(1/\alpha^{2})\log^{2}(m)/(1-p)^{2}
. Then, after
T_{end}\lesssim(\kappa/\eta)\log^{2}\left(1/{\alpha}\right)
iterations, and with an overwhelming probability, we have

\left\lVert U_{t}U_{t}^{\top}-X^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\lesssim d\alpha^{2-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\kappa^{2}dr^{2}\log(1/\alpha)\log^{2}(m)}{(1-p)^{2}m}}\right)}.

(32)

Corollary 3

(Gaussian Noise Model)
.

Suppose that the measurement matrices
\left\{A_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{m}
have i.i.d. standard Gaussian entries, and the noise vector
\mathbf{s}
follows a Gaussian noise model with a variance
\nu_{g}<\infty
(Definition
5
). Consider the iterations of SubGM on
{f}_{\ell_{1}}(U)
with the step-size defined as (
11
), and parameters
\eta\lesssim{1}/\left({\kappa\log(1/\alpha)}\right)
and
\rho=1-\Theta\left({\eta}/({\kappa\log(1/\alpha)})\right)
. Suppose that the initial point
U_{0}
is obtained from Algorithm
2
with an initialization scale that satisfies
\alpha\lesssim 1/\sqrt{d}\wedge\sqrt{dr/m}\wedge 1/\kappa
. Then, after
T_{end}\lesssim({\kappa}/{\eta})\log^{2}\left({1}/{\alpha}\right)
iterations, and with an overwhelming probability, we have

\left\lVert U_{t}U_{t}^{\top}-X^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}=\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\nu_{g}^{2}\kappa^{4}dr^{2}\log^{5}(1/\alpha)\log^{2}(m)}{m}}\right).

(33)

The proof of Corollary
3
follows directly from Theorems
5
and
8
after choosing

\zeta=C\sqrt{\nu_{g}^{2}\kappa^{4}dr^{2}\log^{5}(1/\alpha)\log^{2}(m)/m},

for sufficiently large constant
C
. The details are omitted for brevity.

Remark 1
.

Our result can be readily extended to settings where the measurements are corrupted with both outlier and Gaussian noise values. Consider measurements of the form
y_{i}=\left\langle A_{i},X^{\star}\right\rangle+s^{(1)}_{i}+s^{(2)}_{i}
, where
s^{(1)}_{i}
and
s^{(2)}_{i}
follow the outlier and Gaussian noise models delineated in Definitions
4
and
5
. In this setting, Corollaries
2
and
3
can be combined to show that, with
m=\tilde{\Omega}\left(\nu_{g}^{2}\kappa^{4}dr^{2}/(1-p)^{2}\right)
samples, SubGM with small initialization and geometric step-size achieves the error
\left\lVert U_{t}U_{t}^{\top}-X^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}=\tilde{\mathcal{O}}\left({{\nu_{g}^{2}\kappa^{4}dr^{2}}/({(1-p)^{2}m})}\right)
(modulo logarithmic factors).

7
Concluding Remarks

In this work, we study the performance of sub-gradient method (SubGM) on a nonconvex and nonsmooth formulation of the robust matrix recovery with noisy measurements, where the rank of the true solution
r
is unknown, and over-estimated instead with
r^{\prime}\geq r
. We prove that the over-estimation of the rank has no effect on the performance of SubGM, provided that the initial point is sufficiently close to the origin. Moreover, we prove that SubGM is robust against outlier and Gaussian noise values. In particular, we show that SubGM provably converges to the ground truth, even if the globally optimal solutions of the problem are “spurious”, i.e., they do not correspond to the ground truth. At the heart of our method lies a new notion of restricted isometry property, called Sign-RIP, which guarantees a direction-preserving property for the sub-differentials of the
\ell_{1}
-loss. We show that, while the classical notions of restricted isometry property face major breakdowns in the face of noise, Sign-RIP can handle a wide range of noisy measurements, and hence, is better-suited for analyzing the robust variants of low-rank matrix recovery. A few remarks are in order next:

Spectral vs. random initialization:
In our work, we assume that the initial point is obtained via a special form of the spectral method, followed by a norm reduction. A natural question thus arises as to whether the spectral method can be replaced by small random initialization. Based on our simulations, we observed that SubGM with small random initialization behaves almost the same as SubGM with spectral initialization. Therefore, we conjecture that small random initialization followed by a few iterations of SubGM is in fact equivalent to spectral initialization; a similar result has been recently proven by
Stöger and Soltanolkotabi 2021
for gradient descent on
\ell_{2}
-loss. We consider a rigorous verification of this conjecture as an enticing challenge for future research.

Beyond Sign-RIP:
Another natural question pertains to the performance of SubGM on problems that
do not
satisfy Sign-RIP. An important and relevant example is
over-parameterized matrix completion
, where the linear measurement operator is an element-wise projector that reveals partial and potentially noisy observations of a low-rank matrix. Indeed, the performance SubGM on problems of this type requires a more refined analysis, which is left as future work.

Appendix A
Proofs of the Main Theorems

A.1
Proof of Theorem
6

Before delving into the details, we first present the general overview of our proof technique for Theorem
6
. First, we prove that the conditions of Propositions
2
and
3
hold for every
0\leq t<\infty
. Then, we use the minimum eigenvalue dynamic in Proposition
3
to show that
\lambda_{\min}(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top})\geq 0.98\sigma_{r}
after
\mathcal{O}\left({\log(1/\alpha^{2})}/{(\eta\sigma_{r})}\right)
iterations. In the second phase, we leverage the lower bound
\lambda_{\min}(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top})\geq 0.98\sigma_{r}
to further simplify the one-step dynamics in Proposition
2
, and show that both signal and cross term decay linearly, while the residual term remains in the order of
\alpha^{2}
. This phase lasts for
\mathcal{O}\left({\log(\sigma_{1}/\alpha^{2})}/{(\eta\sigma_{r})}\right)
iterations, and the generalization error can be upper bounded by
\alpha^{2}
at the end of this phase. Finally, in the third phase, we show that the residual term will dominate the signal and cross terms, and the generalization error will decay at a sublinear rate.

Lemma 5
.

The conditions of Propositions
2
and
3
are satisfied for every
0\leq t<\infty
. In particular, for any
0\leq t<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\eta\alpha^{2}t+1},

(34)

\displaystyle\left\lVert S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 1.01\sigma_{1},

(35)

\displaystyle S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}

\displaystyle\succ 0.

(36)

The proof of the above lemma can be found in Appendix
C.3
. Given Lemma
5
, we proceed to prove Theorem
6
.

Phase 1: Eigenvalue Learning.
Due to Proposition
2
and Lemma
5
, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1+2\eta\sigma_{r}-2\eta\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)-2.01\eta\lambda_{\min}^{2}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)

(37)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1+1.99\eta\sigma_{r}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)-2.01\eta\lambda_{\min}^{2}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right),

where we used the assumption
2\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq 2\alpha^{2}\leq 0.01\sigma_{r}
due to our choice of
\alpha
. Now, we consider two cases:

-

Suppose that
T_{1}
is the largest iteration such that
\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\leq\sigma_{r}/2.01
for every
t\leq T_{1}
. According to (
37
), we have

\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\geq(1+0.99\eta\sigma_{r})^{t}\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{0}S_{0}^{\top}\right)\geq(1+0.99\eta\sigma_{r})^{t}\alpha^{2}\sigma_{r}.

This implies that, after
\mathcal{O}\left({\log(1/\alpha^{2})}/{(\eta\sigma_{r})}\right)
iterations, we have
\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)>\sigma_{r}/2.01
, and hence,
T_{1}=\mathcal{O}\left({\log(1/\alpha^{2})}/{(\eta\sigma_{r})}\right)
.

-

For
t>T_{1}
, let
x_{t}=\sigma_{r}-\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)
. Then, according to (
37
), we have

\displaystyle x_{t+1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-2.03\eta\sigma_{r}\right)x_{t}+2.01\eta x_{t}^{2}+0.02\eta\sigma_{r}^{2}

(38)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-1.02\eta\sigma_{r}\right)x_{t}+0.02\eta\sigma_{r}^{2},

where in the second inequality, we used the fact that
x_{t}=\sigma_{r}-\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\leq{1.01\sigma_{r}}/{2.01}
. The above inequality implies

\displaystyle x_{t+1}-0.0196\sigma_{r}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-1.02\eta\sigma_{r}\right)\left(x_{t}-0.0196\sigma_{r}\right)

\displaystyle\implies x_{t+1}-0.0196\sigma_{r}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-1.02\eta\sigma_{r}\right)^{t-T_{1}+1}\left(x_{T_{1}}-0.0196\sigma_{r}\right).

Hence, we have
x_{t}\leq 0.02\sigma_{r}
after
T_{3}=T_{1}+T_{2}
iterations, where
T_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left(1/\eta\sigma_{r}\right)
, which in turn shows that
\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\geq 0.98\sigma_{r}
.

The above analysis shows that
\lambda_{\min}(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top})\geq 0.98\sigma_{r}
for every
t\geq T_{3}=T_{1}+T_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left({\log(1/\alpha^{2})}/{(\eta\sigma_{r})}\right)
.

Phase 2: Global Convergence.
We have
0.98\sigma_{r}\leq\lambda_{\min}(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top})\leq 1.01\sigma_{1}
for every
t\geq T_{3}
. This combined with the one-step signal dynamics (
17
) implies that

\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq\left(1-0.98\eta\sigma_{r}\right)\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+5\eta\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}.

On the other hand, due to Lemma
5
, we have

\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\left\lVert S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq(1.01\sigma_{1})\alpha^{2}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq\left(1-0.98\eta\sigma_{r}\right)\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+6\eta\sigma_{1}\alpha^{2}

(39)

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}\right\rVert-\frac{6\sigma_{1}\alpha^{2}}{0.98\sigma_{r}}\leq\left(1-0.98\eta\sigma_{r}\right)\left(\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert-\frac{6\sigma_{1}\alpha^{2}}{0.98\sigma_{r}}\right)

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}\right\rVert-\frac{6\sigma_{1}\alpha^{2}}{0.98\sigma_{r}}\leq\left(1-0.98\eta\sigma_{r}\right)^{t-T_{3}+1}\left(\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{T_{3}}S_{T_{3}}^{\top}\right\rVert-\frac{6\sigma_{1}\alpha^{2}}{0.98\sigma_{r}}\right).

Therefore,

\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq{7\kappa\alpha^{2}}\quad\text{for}\quad t\geq T_{5}=T_{3}+T_{4},\quad\text{where}\quad T_{4}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\kappa\alpha^{2}}\right)}{\eta\sigma_{r}}\right).

Here, we use the inequality
\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{T_{3}}S_{T_{3}}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert+\left\lVert S_{T_{3}}S_{T_{3}}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq 2.01\sigma_{1}
. On the other hand, the one-step dynamics for the cross term (
18
) implies that

\displaystyle\left\lVert S_{t+1}E_{t+1}^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)+2\eta\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+2\eta\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\right)\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-0.5\eta\sigma_{r}\right)\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\implies\left\lVert S_{t+1}E_{t+1}^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-0.5\eta\sigma_{r}\right)^{t-T_{5}+1}\left\lVert S_{T_{5}}E_{T_{5}}^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-0.5\eta\sigma_{r}\right)^{t-T_{5}+1}\left(1.01\alpha\sqrt{\sigma_{1}}\right),

(40)

where the second inequality follows from the proven upper bound
\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq{7\kappa\alpha^{2}}
and Lemma
5
. Moreover, the last inequality is due to the fact that

\left\lVert S_{T_{5}}E_{T_{5}}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert S_{T_{5}}\right\rVert\left\lVert E_{T_{5}}\right\rVert\leq 1.01\alpha\sqrt{\sigma_{1}}.

The inequality (
40
) results in

\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq\alpha^{2}\quad\text{for}\quad t\geq T_{7}=T_{6}+T_{5}\quad\text{where}\quad T_{6}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\left(\frac{\sqrt{\sigma_{1}}}{\alpha}\right)}{\eta\sigma_{r}}\right).

This upper bound can in turn be used in (
39
) to further strengthen the upper bound on the signal term as follows

\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq{\alpha^{2}}\qquad\text{for}\qquad t\geq T_{8}=T_{7}+T_{6}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)}{\eta\sigma_{r}}\right).

Finally, invoking the signal-residual decomposition in Lemma
3
, we have

\left\lVert U_{t}U_{t}^{\top}-X^{*}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right\rVert+2\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\lesssim\alpha^{2}\ \ \text{for}\ t\geq T_{8}=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\alpha^{2}}\right)}{\eta\sigma_{r}}\right).

Phase 3: Sublinear convergence.
Once both signal and cross terms are in the order of
\alpha^{2}
, the residual term becomes the dominant term, while both signal and cross terms maintain their linear decay rates. Therefore, we have

\left\lVert U_{t}U_{t}^{\top}-X^{*}\right\rVert\lesssim\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\lesssim\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\eta\alpha^{2}t+1}.

This completes the proof.
\hfill\square

A.2
Proof of Theorem
7

The proof of Theorem
7
follows the same structure as the proof of Theorem
6
: first, we use Proposition
4
to show that
\lambda_{\min}(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top})
reaches
0.98\sigma_{r}
after
T_{1}\lesssim{\log(1/\alpha)}/{(\eta\sigma_{r}\underline{\varphi}^{2})}
iterations. Given this inequality and equipped with the one-step dynamics of the signal, cross, and residual terms (Propositions
5
and
6
), we then establish the linear convergence of SubGM to the ground truth. As a first step, we show an important property of the proposed initialization scheme.

Lemma 6
.

Suppose that the measurements are noiseless and satisfy
\left(4r,\delta,\varepsilon,\mathcal{S}\right)
-Sign-RIP where
\varepsilon=0
, and
X^{\star}\in\mathcal{S}
. Then, the initial point
U_{0}=VS_{0}+V_{\perp}E_{0}
generated from Algorithm
2
satisfies

\left\lVert U_{0}U_{0}^{\top}-\alpha^{2}\varphi(X^{\star})\frac{X^{\star}}{\left\lVert X^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}}\right\rVert\leq 2\alpha^{2}\varphi(X^{\star})\delta,

(41)

The proof can be found in Appendix
D.5
. An immediate consequence of the above lemma is the following inequality:

\left\lVert S_{0}S_{0}^{\top}-\alpha^{2}\varphi(X^{\star})\frac{\Sigma}{\left\lVert X^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}}\right\rVert\vee\left\lVert S_{0}E_{0}^{\top}\right\rVert\vee\left\lVert E_{0}E_{0}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq 2\alpha^{2}\varphi(X^{\star})\delta.

(42)

Given this property of the proposed initialization scheme,
we next show that the conditions of Propositions
4
,
5
, and
6
are satisfied throughout solution path.

Lemma 7
.

We either have
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}\lesssim d\alpha^{2-\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{r}\kappa^{2}\delta)}
for some
0\leq t\leq T_{end}
, or the conditions of Propositions
4
,
5
, and
6
are satisfied for every
0\leq t\leq T_{end}
. In particular, for any
0\leq t\leq T_{end}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert F_{t}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\eta\bar{\varphi}^{2}\left(100\sqrt{r}\sigma_{1}^{1.5}\delta+30\sigma_{1}\sqrt{\alpha\bar{\varphi}\delta}\right)(t+1),

(43)

\displaystyle\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{1-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{r}\kappa^{2}\delta\right)}\sqrt{\bar{\varphi}\delta},

(44)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 5\sigma_{1},

(45)

\displaystyle\left\lVert S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 1.01\sigma_{1},

(46)

\displaystyle S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}

\displaystyle\succ 0,

(47)

\displaystyle\left\lVert E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 1/3.

(48)

The proof of Lemma
7
is provided in Appendix
D.6
. Note that the inequality
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}\lesssim d\alpha^{2-\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{r}\kappa^{2}\delta)}
for some
0\leq t\leq T_{end}
readily implies the final result. On the the other hand, if
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}\gtrsim d\alpha^{2-\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{r}\kappa^{2}\delta)}
, Lemma
7
implies that Propositions
4
,
5
, and
6
hold for every
0\leq t\leq T_{end}
.

Phase 1: Eigenvalue Learning.
Based on Lemma
7
, the conditions of Proposition
4
are satisfied for
T_{1}\lesssim{\log(1/\alpha)}/{(\eta\sigma_{r}\underline{\varphi}^{2})}
, and we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}\right)\geq

\displaystyle\left(\left(1+2\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}\right)-2\bar{\eta}_{t}\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert-72\bar{\eta}_{t}\delta\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle-2\bar{\eta}_{t}\left(1+\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{2}.

(49)

Due to (
43
) and (
44
), we have
\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert F_{t}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 0.005\sigma_{r}
for every
t\lesssim{\log\left({1}/{\alpha}\right)}/{(\eta\sigma_{r}\underline{\varphi}^{2})}
, where we used Lemma
7
and the assumed upper bounds on
\delta
and
\alpha
. Moreover,
72\delta\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}\leq 150\sigma_{1}\delta\leq 0.005{\sigma_{r}}
, where again we used the assumed upper bound on
\delta
and Lemma
7
. These two inequalities together with (
A.2
) lead to

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}\right)\geq\left(1+1.99\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)-2.01\bar{\eta}_{t}\lambda_{\min}^{2}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right).

The above inequality is identical to (
37
), after noticing that
\eta\underline{\varphi}^{2}\leq\bar{\eta}_{t}\leq\eta\bar{\varphi}^{2}
. On the other hand, Lemma
6
shows that
\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{0}S_{0}^{\top}\right)\geq\alpha^{2}\varphi(X^{\star})\left(\frac{\sigma_{r}}{\left\lVert X^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}}-2\delta\right)\geq\alpha^{2}\underline{\varphi}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{r}\kappa}
. Therefore, using an argument analogous to the proof of Theorem
6
, we have
\lambda_{\min}(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top})\geq 0.98\sigma_{r}
for
t\geq T_{1}=\mathcal{O}(\log(1/\alpha)/(\eta\sigma_{r}\underline{\varphi}^{2}))
. The details are omitted for brevity.

Phase 2: Global convergence.
Recall the signal-residual decomposition

\displaystyle\left\lVert U_{t}U_{t}^{\top}-X^{\star}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+2\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+\left\lVert F_{t}F_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+\left\lVert G_{t}G_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert.

In what follows, we show that once
\lambda_{\min}(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top})\geq 0.98\sigma_{r}
, all terms in the above inequality decay at a linear rate, except for
\left\lVert G_{t}G_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert
. To this goal, first note that Propositions
5
and
6
together with
0.98\sigma_{r}\leq\lambda_{\min}(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top})\leq 1.01\sigma_{1}
lead to the following one-step dynamics:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-0.98\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}\right)\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+5\bar{\eta}_{t}\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}+37\bar{\eta}_{t}\delta\bar{\varphi}^{2}\sigma_{1}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F},

(50)

\displaystyle\left\lVert S_{t+1}E_{t+1}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-0.98\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}+2\bar{\eta}_{t}\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+2\bar{\eta}_{t}\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}\right\rVert\right)\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+22\bar{\eta}_{t}\delta\sigma_{1}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F},

(51)

\displaystyle\left\lVert F_{t+1}\right\rVert\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-0.98\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}\right)\left\lVert F_{t}\right\rVert+5\sqrt{\sigma_{1}}\bar{\eta}_{t}\delta\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}+6\bar{\eta}_{t}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert.

(52)

Note that, unlike
\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert
and
\left\lVert F_{t}\right\rVert
, the cross term
\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert
enjoys linear decay only under the condition
\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert<0.98\sigma_{r}
, which is not necessarily satisfied in the eigenvalue learning phase. Our next lemma shows that this condition is satisfied, shortly after the eigenvalue learning phase.

Lemma 8
.

We have
\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert<0.03\sigma_{r}
, for every
T_{end}\geq t\geq T_{1}+T_{2}
, where
T_{2}=\mathcal{O}\left({\log(\kappa)}/{(\eta\sigma_{r}\underline{\varphi}^{2}}\right)
.

Proof.

It is easy to see that

\displaystyle 5\bar{\eta}_{t}\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 5.05\sigma_{1}\bar{\eta}_{t}\left\lVert E_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 5.05\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{1}\left(\left\lVert F_{t}\right\rVert+\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert\right)^{2}\leq 0.01\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}^{2},

(53)

where the last inequality is due to our choice of
\delta
and Lemma
7
. Similarly, we can show that
37\bar{\eta}_{t}\delta\sigma_{1}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}\leq 0.01\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}^{2}
. These two inequalities combined with (
50
) lead to

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-0.98\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}\right)\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+0.02\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}^{2}.

This implies that
\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq 0.03\sigma_{r}
after
\mathcal{O}\left({\log(\kappa)}/{(\eta\sigma_{r}\underline{\varphi}^{2})}\right)
iterations.
∎

The above lemma shows that the one-step dynamic of the cross term can be simplified as

\displaystyle\left\lVert S_{t+1}E_{t+1}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-0.49\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}\right)\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+22\bar{\eta}_{t}\delta^{2}\sigma_{1}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}.

(54)

Moreover, recall that

\displaystyle\left\lVert G_{t+1}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\bar{\eta}_{t}^{2}\left(2\left\lVert E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert E_{t}\right\rVert^{4}+2\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\left\lVert E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\right)+7\bar{\eta}_{t}\delta\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}\right)\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+5\bar{\eta}_{t}^{2}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert^{2}+7\bar{\eta}_{t}\delta\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}\right)\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert.

(55)

Here we use the fact that
\left\lVert E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\vee\left\lVert E_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert
.
Now, let us define
\gamma_{t}:=\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+2\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+\left\lVert F_{t}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}
as an upper bound for the generalization error
\left\lVert U_{t}U_{t}^{\top}-X^{\star}\right\rVert
. Combining (
50
), (
52
), (
54
), and (
55
), we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}\right\rVert+2\left\lVert S_{t+1}E_{t+1}^{\top}\right\rVert+\left\lVert F_{t+1}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert G_{t+1}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-C_{1}\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}\right)\gamma_{t}+C_{2}\bar{\eta}_{t}\delta\sigma_{1}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}+C_{3}\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-C_{1}\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}+C_{2}\bar{\eta}_{t}\sqrt{r}\sigma_{1}\delta\right)\gamma_{t}+C_{4}\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-C_{5}\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}\right)\gamma_{t}+C_{4}\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2},

for some universal constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3},C_{4},C_{5}>0
. This implies that

\displaystyle\gamma_{t+1}-C_{6}\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\left(1-C_{5}\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}\right)\left(\gamma_{t}-C_{6}\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\gamma_{t+1}-C_{6}\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\left(1-C_{5}\bar{\eta}_{t}\sigma_{r}\right)^{t-(T_{1}+T_{2})}(\gamma_{T_{1}+T_{2}}-C_{6}\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}).

Note that
\gamma_{T_{1}+T_{2}}\leq 0.1\sigma_{r}
, according to Lemma
8
, inequality (
53
), and the upper bounds on
\left\lVert F_{t}\right\rVert^{2}
and
\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}
. On the other hand,
\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert\leq\alpha^{1-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{r}\kappa^{2}\delta\right)}\sqrt{\bar{\varphi}\delta}
, according to Lemma
7
. Therefore, after
T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}
iterations with
T_{3}=\mathcal{O}\left({\log(1/\alpha)}/{(\eta\sigma_{r}\underline{\varphi}^{2})}\right)
, we have
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{d}\gamma_{t}\lesssim{d}\alpha^{2-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{r}\kappa^{2}\delta\right)}{\bar{\varphi}\delta}
. This completes the proof.
\hfill\square

A.3
Proof of Theorem
8

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}\geq\zeta
for every
0\leq t\leq T_{end}
; otherwise, the final bound for the generalization error holds and the proof is complete. Before delving into the details, we first provide a general overview of our approach. The proof of Theorem
8
is similar to that of Theorem
7
with a key difference that we divide our analysis into two parts depending on the value of
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert
: if
\eta\rho^{t}/\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\lesssim 1/\sigma_{1}
, then
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert
decays slower than
\sigma_{1}\eta\rho^{t}
. Under this assumption, we will use the one-step dynamics of signal, cross, and residual terms in Propositions
7
,
8
, and
9
to prove that
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert
decays exponentially fast. Alternatively,
\eta\rho^{t}/\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\gtrsim 1/\sigma_{1}
implies that
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\lesssim\sigma_{1}\eta\rho^{t}
, which readily establishes the exponential decay of the generalization error. Indeed, the above two cases may occur alternatively, which requires a more delicate analysis.

Similar to the proof of Theorem
7
, we show that SubGM undergoes two phases: (1) eigenvalue learning phase, and (2) global convergence phase. In the first phase, we show that
\lambda_{\min}(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top})
and
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert
converge to
0.98\sigma_{r}
and
0.02\sigma_{r}
, respectively. The main difference between the proofs of Theorems
7
and
8
is the fact that the one-step dynamics of signal, cross, and residual terms may not hold during the entire solution trajectory. However, our next lemma shows that they indeed hold in the first phase of our analysis.

Lemma 9
.

Suppose that
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\geq 0.02\sigma_{r}
for every
0\leq t\leq\bar{T}\leq T_{end}
. Then, the assumptions of Propositions
7
,
8
, and
9
are satisfied for every
0\leq t\leq\bar{T}
. In particular, for any
0\leq t\leq\bar{T}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert F_{t}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left(15\sqrt{r}\eta\rho^{t}\sqrt{\sigma_{1}}\delta+12\sqrt{2}\eta\sqrt{\bar{\varphi}\alpha\delta}\right)(t+1),

(56)

\displaystyle\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{2}\alpha^{1-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{r}\kappa\delta\right)}\sqrt{\bar{\varphi}\delta},

(57)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 5\sigma_{1},

(58)

\displaystyle\left\lVert S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 1.1\sigma_{1},

(59)

\displaystyle S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}

\displaystyle\succ 0,

(60)

\displaystyle\left\lVert E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 1/3.

(61)

The proof of Lemma
9
is analogous to that of Lemma
7
, and can be found in Appendix
E.5
. Equipped with this lemma, we next provide the proof for the eigenvalue learning phase.

Phase 1: Eigenvalue Learning.

We will show that
\lambda_{\min}(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top})\geq 0.98\sigma_{r}
within
t\leq T_{1}=\mathcal{O}((\kappa/\eta)\log(1/\alpha))
iterations. After that, we prove that we only need
T_{2}=\mathcal{O}((\kappa/\eta)\log(\kappa))
additional iterations to ensure that
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\leq 0.02\sigma_{r}
; this marks the end of Phase 1. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\geq 0.02\sigma_{r}
for every
t\leq T_{1}
. To see this, suppose that
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\leq 0.02\sigma_{r}
for some
t\leq T_{1}
. This implies that
\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\leq 0.02\sigma_{r}
, which in turn leads to
\lambda_{\min}(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top})\geq 0.98\sigma_{r}
. On the other hand,
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\geq 0.02\sigma_{r}
together with Lemma
9
implies that the one-step dynamic in Proposition
7
holds and we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}\right)\geq

\displaystyle\left(\left(1+\frac{\eta\rho^{t}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\sigma_{r}\right)^{2}-\frac{2\eta\rho^{t}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert-384\sqrt{r}\eta\rho^{t}\delta\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle-2\frac{\eta\rho^{t}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\left(1+\frac{\eta\rho^{t}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\sigma_{r}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{2}.

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{2\eta\rho^{t}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\left(\sigma_{r}-\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{r}\sigma_{1}\delta)\right)\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)-\frac{2.01\eta\rho^{t}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{1.99\eta\rho^{t}\sigma_{r}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)-\frac{2.01\eta\rho^{t}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{2}

(62)

where the second inequality follows from
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\geq 0.02\sigma_{r}
and
\eta\lesssim 1
, and the last inequality follows from
\delta\lesssim 1/(\sqrt{r}\sigma_{1})
. The rest of the proof for the eigenvalue learning phase is similar to the arguments made after (
37
) in the proof of Theorem
6
. Suppose that
T^{\prime}_{1}
is the largest iteration such that
\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\leq\sigma_{r}/2.01
for every
t\leq T_{1}^{\prime}
. We show that
T_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}((\kappa/\eta)\log(1/\alpha))
. To see this, note that for every
t\leq T_{1}^{\prime}
, the above inequality can be simplified as

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1+\frac{0.99\eta\rho^{t}\sigma_{r}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{0}S_{0}^{\top}\right)\prod_{s=0}^{t}\left(1+\frac{\eta\rho^{s}}{6\kappa}\right),

(63)

where we used the assumption
\delta\lesssim 1/(\sqrt{r}\kappa)
and
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\leq 5\sigma_{1}
. To proceed with the proof, we need the following technical lemma.

Lemma 10
.

For any
\alpha>0,0<\rho<1
and
T\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we have

\exp\left(\frac{\alpha T\rho^{T}}{1+\alpha}\right)\leq\prod_{t=0}^{T}\left(1+\alpha\rho^{t}\right)\leq\exp\left(\frac{\alpha}{1-\rho}\right).

(64)

The proof of Lemma
10
can be found in Appendix
F.5
. Lemma
10
together with Lemma
6
and (
63
) leads to

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\left(\frac{\eta t\rho^{t}}{6\kappa+\eta}\right)\alpha^{2}\varphi(X^{\star})\left(\frac{\sigma_{r}}{\left\lVert X^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}}-\delta\right)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\left(\frac{\eta t\rho^{t}}{6\kappa+\eta}\right)\alpha^{2}\varphi(X^{\star})\left(\frac{\sigma_{r}}{\sqrt{r}\sigma_{1}}-\delta\right)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\left(\frac{\eta t\rho^{t}}{6\kappa+\eta}\right)\frac{\alpha^{2}\varphi(X^{\star})}{2\sqrt{r}\kappa}.

Due to our assumption
\rho=1-\Theta(\eta/(\kappa\log(1/\alpha)))
, we have
\rho^{t}\geq 1-\mathcal{O}(\eta t/(\kappa\log(1/\alpha)))
. This together with the assumption
t\leq\mathcal{O}((\kappa/\eta)\log(1/\alpha))
leads to
\rho^{t}\geq 1-\mathcal{O}(1)\geq\Omega(1)
. Therefore, one can write

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\geq\exp\left(\Omega(1)\frac{\eta t}{\kappa}\right)\frac{\alpha^{2}\varphi(X^{\star})}{2\sqrt{r}\kappa}.

Given the above equation, it is easy to verify that after
\mathcal{O}((\kappa/\eta)\log(1/\alpha))
iterations, we have
\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\geq\sigma_{r}/2.01
. This implies that
T_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}((\kappa/\eta)\log(1/\alpha))
.

For
t>T_{1}^{\prime}
, define
x_{t}=\sigma_{r}-\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)
. Then, arguments analogous to the proof of Theorem
6
can be used to write

\displaystyle x_{t+1}-0.0196\sigma_{r}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-1.02\frac{\eta\rho^{t}\sigma_{r}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\right)(x_{t}-0.0196\sigma_{r})

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta\rho^{t}}{5\kappa}\right)(x_{t}-0.0196\sigma_{r})

\displaystyle\leq(x_{T_{1}^{\prime}}-0.0196\sigma_{r})\prod_{s=T_{1}^{\prime}}^{t}\left(1-\frac{\eta\rho^{t}}{5\kappa}\right).

Recall that
\rho^{t}=\Omega(1)
for every
t\leq\mathcal{O}((\kappa/\eta)\log(1/\alpha))
. Therefore, for every
T_{1}^{\prime}<t\leq\mathcal{O}((\kappa/\eta)\log(1/\alpha))
, we have

\displaystyle x_{t+1}-0.0196\sigma_{r}

\displaystyle\leq(x_{T_{1}^{\prime}}-0.0196\sigma_{r})\left(1-\Omega(1)\frac{\eta\sigma_{r}}{\kappa}\right)^{t-T_{1}^{\prime}+1}.

This in turn implies that after additional
T_{1}^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{O}(\kappa/\eta)
iterations, we have
x_{t+1}\leq 0.02\sigma_{r}
. Therefore, we have
\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\geq 0.98\sigma_{r}
after
T_{1}=T_{1}^{\prime}+T_{1}^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{O}((\kappa/\eta)\log(1/\alpha))
iterations. Now, it suffices to show that, after additional
T_{2}=\mathcal{O}((\kappa/\eta)\log(\kappa))
iterations, we have
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\leq 0.02\sigma_{r}
. To this goal, suppose that
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\geq 0.02\sigma_{r}
for every
T_{1}\leq t\leq T_{1}+T_{2}
. Recalling the signal-residual decomposition (
21
), one can write

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+2\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+\left\lVert F_{t}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+0.01\sigma_{r},

where the second inequality follows from Lemma
9
. Given the above inequality,
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\geq 0.02\sigma_{r}
implies that
\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\geq 0.01\sigma_{r}
for every
T_{1}\leq t\leq T_{1}+T_{2}
. Combined with the one-step dynamic for the signal term, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta\rho^{t}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\right)\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+5\frac{\eta\rho^{t}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}+193\sqrt{r}\eta\rho^{t}\delta\sigma_{1}

(65)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\left(1-\frac{0.98\eta\rho^{t}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\sigma_{r}\right)\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{r}\eta\rho^{t}\delta\sigma_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert-0.98\eta\rho^{t}\sigma_{r}\frac{\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+0.01\sigma_{r}}+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{r}\eta\rho^{t}\delta\sigma_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert-0.49\eta\rho^{t}\sigma_{r}+\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{r}\eta\rho^{t}\delta\sigma_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert-\Omega\left(\eta\rho^{t}\sigma_{r}\right),

where in (a) we used the fact that
\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\lesssim\sqrt{\sigma_{1}}\left\lVert F_{t}\right\rVert\lesssim\sigma_{1}\sqrt{r}\eta_{0}\rho^{t}\delta t
,
\delta\lesssim{1}/\left({\sqrt{r}\kappa^{2}\bar{\varphi}^{4}\log^{2}\left(1/\alpha\right)}\right)
, and
t\lesssim({\kappa}/{\eta})\log\left({1}/{\alpha}\right)
. The above inequality leads to

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{T_{1}}S_{T_{1}}^{\top}\right\rVert-\Omega\left(\sum_{s=T_{1}}^{t-1}\eta\rho^{t}\sigma_{r}\right).

Therefore, after
T_{3}=T_{1}+\mathcal{O}((\kappa/\eta)\log\kappa)
iterations, we have
\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq 0.01\sigma_{r}
. This completes the proof of the first phase.

Phase 2: Global Convergence.

In the second phase, we show that, once
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\leq 0.02\sigma_{r}
,
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert
starts to decay linearly until it is dominated by
\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}
. Similar to before, we define
\gamma_{t}=\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+2\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+\left\lVert F_{t}\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}
. Our next lemma plays a central role in our subsequent arguments:

Lemma 11
.

Suppose that
T_{3}\leq t\leq T_{end}
is chosen such that

\gamma_{s}\leq 0.1\sigma_{r}
and
\left\lVert\Delta_{s}\right\rVert\geq\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{s}\right\rVert^{2}
, for every
T_{3}\leq s\leq t
. Then, we have

-

\gamma_{t+1}\leq 0.1\sigma_{r}
.

Moreover, at least one of the following statements are satisfied:

-

\left\lVert\Delta_{t+1}\right\rVert\geq\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{t+1}\right\rVert^{2}
,

-

\left\lVert\Delta_{t+1}\right\rVert\lesssim\sqrt{d}\alpha^{2-\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{r}\kappa\delta)}
.

To streamline the presentation, we defer the proof of the above lemma to Appendix
F.6
. According to the above lemma, we may assume that
\gamma_{t}\leq 0.1\sigma_{r}
and
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\geq\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}
for all iterations
T_{3}\leq t\leq T_{end}
; otherwise, we have
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\lesssim\sqrt{d}\alpha^{2-\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{r}\kappa\delta)}
for some
T_{3}\leq t\leq T_{end}
, which readily completes the proof. On the other hand, the assumptions
\gamma_{t}\leq 0.1\sigma_{r}
and
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\geq\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}
lead to

\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\geq 0.9\sigma_{r},\quad\left\lVert S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq 1.1\sigma_{1},\quad\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq 0.1\sigma_{r},\quad\left\lVert E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\right\rVert\leq 0.2.

Together with our analysis in Phase 1, this implies that the one-step dynamic of
G_{t}
holds for every
0\leq t\leq T_{end}
, and we have

\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert\leq 2\sqrt{2}\alpha^{1-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{r}\kappa\delta\right)}\sqrt{\bar{\varphi}\delta}
, for every
0\leq t\leq T_{end}
.

Under the assumption
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\geq\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}
, our next lemma shows that, if
\left\lVert\Delta_{t+T_{3}}\right\rVert\geq 0.02\sigma_{r}\rho^{t}
for some
t\geq 0
, then there exists
t^{\prime}
satisfying
t^{\prime}-t=\mathcal{O}(1/\eta)
such that
\left\lVert\Delta_{t^{\prime}+T_{3}}\right\rVert\leq 0.02\sigma_{r}\rho^{t^{\prime}}
. This in turn ensures that the generalization error decays by a constant factor every
\mathcal{O}(1/\eta)
iterations until it reaches the same order as
\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}
. In particular, we have the following lemma:

Lemma 12
.

Suppose that
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\geq\sqrt{d}\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}
for every
T_{3}\leq t\leq T_{end}
. Suppose that
t_{0}\geq 1
satisfies
\left\lVert\Delta_{t_{0}+T_{3}-1}\right\rVert\leq 0.02\sigma_{r}\rho^{t_{0}-1}
and
\left\lVert\Delta_{t_{0}+T_{3}}\right\rVert>0.02\sigma_{r}\rho^{t_{0}}
. Then, after at most
\Delta t=\mathcal{O}\left(1/\eta\right)
iterations, we have
\left\lVert\Delta_{t_{0}+\Delta t+T_{3}}\right\rVert\leq 0.02\sigma_{r}\rho^{t_{0}+\Delta t}
.

The proof of the above lemma is presented in Appendix
F.7
. We show how Lemma
12
can be used to finish the proof of Theorem
8
. Recall that
\rho=1-\Theta\left(\eta/(\kappa\log(1/\alpha))\right)
. Let us pick
T_{4}=\mathcal{O}((\kappa/\eta)\log^{2}(1/\alpha))
. Simple calculation reveals that
0.02\sigma_{r}\rho^{T_{4}}\lesssim\alpha^{2}
. According to Lemma
12
, if
\left\lVert\Delta_{T_{4}+T_{3}}\right\rVert>0.02\sigma_{r}\rho^{T_{4}}
, then there exists
\Delta t=\mathcal{O}\left(1/\eta\right)
such that
\left\lVert\Delta_{T_{4}+T_{3}+\Delta t}\right\rVert\leq 0.02\sigma_{r}\rho^{T_{4}+\Delta t}\lesssim\alpha^{2}
. Combined with Lemma
11
, we have
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{d}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\lesssim d\left\lVert G_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\vee\zeta\lesssim d\alpha^{2-\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{r}\kappa\delta\right)}\vee\zeta
after at most
T_{end}=T_{3}+T_{4}+\Delta t=\mathcal{O}((\kappa/\eta)\log^{2}(1/\alpha))
iterations. This completes the proof of Theorem
8
.
\hfill\square

Appendix B
Proofs of Sign-RIP

B.1
Preliminary

We first provide the preliminary probability tools for proving Theorems
4
and
5
.

Definition 6

(Sub-Gaussian random variable)
.

We say a random variable
X\in\mathbb{R}
with expectation
\mathbb{E}[X]=\mu
is
\sigma^{2}
-sub-Gaussian if for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\lambda(X-\mu)}\right]\leq e^{\frac{\lambda^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}
.
Moreover, the sub-Gaussian norm of
X
is defined as
\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}:=\sup_{p\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}\left\{p^{-1/2}(\mathbb{E}[|X|^{p}])^{1/p}\right\}
.

According to
[
36
]
, the following statements are equivalent:

•

X
is
\sigma^{2}
-sub-Gaussian.

•

(Tail bound) For any
t>0
, we have
\mathbb{P}(|X-\mu|\geq t)\leq 2e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}
.

•

(Moment bound) We have
\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\sigma
.

Next, we provide the definitions of the sub-Gaussian process,
\xi
-net, and covering number.

Definition 7

(Sub-Gaussian process)
.

A zero mean stochastic process
\{\mathcal{X}_{\theta},\theta\in\mathbb{T}\}
is a
\sigma^{2}
-sub-Gaussian process with respect to a metric
d
on a set
\mathbb{T}
, if for every
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{T}
, the random variable
\mathcal{X}_{\theta}-\mathcal{X}_{\theta^{\prime}}
is
\left(\sigma d(\theta,\theta^{\prime})\right)^{2}
-sub-Gaussian.

Definition 8

(
\xi
-net and covering number)
.

A set
\mathcal{N}
is called an
\xi
-net on
(\mathbb{T},d)
if for every
t\in\mathbb{T}
, there exists
\pi(t)\in\mathcal{N}
such that
d(t,\pi(t))\leq\xi
. The covering number
N(\mathbb{T},d,\xi)
is defined as the smallest cardinality of an
\xi
-net for
(\mathbb{T},d)
:

N(\mathbb{T},d,\xi):=\inf\{|\mathcal{N}|:\mathcal{N}\text{ is an }\xi\text{-net for }(\mathbb{T},d)\}.

Next, we introduce some additional notations which will be used throughout our arguments. Define the rank-
k
and
\varepsilon
-approximate rank-
k
unit balls as:

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{k}

\displaystyle=\{X\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}:\mathrm{rank}(X)\leq k,\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}=1\},

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\{X\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}:\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}=1\text{ and }\exists X^{\prime}\text{ such that }\mathrm{rank}(X^{\prime})\leq k,\left\lVert X-X^{\prime}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\varepsilon\}.

For simplicity of notation, we use
N_{k,\xi}
to denote
N(\mathbb{S}_{k},\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{F},\xi)
. Moreover, we define
\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}
as the restriction of the set
\mathcal{S}
to the set of
\varepsilon
-approximate rank-
k
matrices, i.e.,
\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}=\{X:X\in\mathcal{S},X\text{ is }\varepsilon\text{-approximate rank-}k\}
.
The following lemma characterizes the covering number of the set of low-rank matrices with unit norm.

Lemma 13

(
Li et al. 2020a
)
.

We have
N_{k,\xi}=N(\mathbb{S}_{k},\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{F},\xi)\leq\left(\frac{9}{\xi}\right)^{(2d+1)k}
.

The following well-known result characterizes a concentration bound on the supremum of a sub-Gaussian process.

Theorem 9

(Corollary 5.25 and Theorem 5.29 in
[
35
]
)
.

Let
\{X_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{T}}
be a separable sub-Gaussian process on
(\mathbb{T},d)
. Then, the following statements hold:

•

We have

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in\mathbb{T}}X_{t}\right]\leq 12\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log N(\mathbb{T},d,\xi)}d\xi.

•

For all
t_{0}\in\mathbb{T}
and
x\geq 0
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\in\mathbb{T}}\left\{X_{t}-X_{t_{0}}\right\}\geq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log N(\mathbb{T},d,\xi)}d\xi+x\right)\leq Ce^{-x^{2}/C\operatorname{diam}(\mathbb{T})^{2}},

where
C<\infty
is a universal constant, and
\operatorname{diam}(\mathbb{T})=\sup_{t,t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{T}}\{d(t,t^{\prime})\}
is the diameter of
\mathbb{T}
.

Equipped with these preliminary results, we proceed with the proof of Theorem
4
.

B.2
Proof of Theorem
4

To provide the proof of Theorem
4
, we first define the following stochastic process:

\mathcal{H}_{X,Y}=\sup\left\{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathrm{Sign}(\left\langle A_{i},X\right\rangle-s_{i})\left\langle A_{i},Y\right\rangle-\varphi(X)\left\langle\frac{X}{\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}},Y\right\rangle\right\},

where
\varphi(X):=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\left(1-p+p\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-s^{2}/(2\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}^{2})}\right]\right)
, and the supremum is taken over the set-valued function
\mathrm{Sign}(\cdot)
. Moreover, to streamline the presentation and whenever there is no ambiguity, we drop the supremum when it is taken with respect to the set-valued function
\mathrm{Sign}(\cdot)
. Our next lemma provides a sufficient condition for Sign-RIP.

Lemma 14
.

Sign-RIP holds with parameters delineated in Theorem
4
, if

\sup_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon},Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}}\mathcal{H}_{X,Y}\lesssim\inf_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}}\varphi(X)\delta.

(66)

Proof.

According to the definition, Sign-RIP is satisfied if, for every
X,Y\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}
and
Q\in\mathcal{Q}(X)
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle Q-\varphi(X)\frac{X}{\|X\|_{F}},\frac{Y}{\left\lVert Y\right\rVert_{F}}\right\rangle\leq\varphi(X)\delta.

(67)

Recall that
\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}=\{X:\zeta\leq\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}\leq R,X\text{ is }\varepsilon\text{-approximate rank-}k\}
. This implies that
Y\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}
if
Y/\left\lVert Y\right\rVert_{F}\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}
. Hence, it suffices to restrict
Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}
. Therefore, Sign-RIP is satisfied if

\mathcal{H}_{X,Y}\leq\varphi(X)\delta,\quad\forall X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon},Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}.

Hence, to guarantee Sign-RIP, it suffices to have

\sup_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon},Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}}\mathcal{H}_{X,Y}\leq\inf_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}}\varphi(X)\delta.

∎

Relying on the above lemma, we instead focus on analyzing the stochastic process
\{\mathcal{H}_{X,Y}\}_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon},Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}}
. As a first step towards this goal, we show that the scaling function can be used to characterize
\mathbb{E}[\left\langle Q,Y\right\rangle]
, for every
Q\in\mathcal{Q}(X)
.

Lemma 15
.

Suppose that the matrix
A
has i.i.d. standard Gaussian entries and the noise satisfies Assumption
4
. Then, for every
Q\in\mathcal{Q}(X)
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle Q,Y\right\rangle\right]=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\left(1-p+p\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-s^{2}/\left(2\left\|X\right\|_{F}\right)}\right]\right)\left\langle\frac{X}{\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}},Y\right\rangle.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}=\left\lVert Y\right\rVert_{F}=1
. Let us denote
u:=\left\langle A,X\right\rangle,v:=\left\langle A,Y\right\rangle,\rho:=\Cov(u,v)=\left\langle X,Y\right\rangle
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Sign}\left(\left\langle A,X\right\rangle-s\right)\left\langle A,Y\right\rangle|s\not=0\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Sign}\left(u-s\right)v|s\not=0\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\rho\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Sign}(u-s)u|s\not=0\right]

\displaystyle=\rho\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{s}^{\infty}u\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-u^{2}/2}du-\int_{-\infty}^{s}u\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-u^{2}/2}du\right]

\displaystyle=\rho\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{s}^{\infty}u\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-u^{2}/2}du+\int_{-s}^{\infty}u\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-u^{2}/2}du\right]

\displaystyle=2\rho\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{|s|}^{\infty}u\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-u^{2}/2}du\right]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\mathbb{P}}\left[\int_{|s|}^{\infty}d\left(-e^{-u^{2}/2}\right)\right]\left\langle X,Y\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\mathbb{P}}\left[e^{-s^{2}/2}\right]\left\langle X,Y\right\rangle,

where, in (a), we used the fact that
v|u,s\sim\mathcal{N}(\rho u,1-\rho^{2})
since
s
is independent of
u,v
. Similarly, one can show that

\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Sign}\left(\left\langle A,X\right\rangle\right)\left\langle A,Y\right\rangle\right]=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\left\langle X,Y\right\rangle
. The proof is completed by noting that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Sign}\left(s+\left\langle A,X\right\rangle\right)\left\langle A,Y\right\rangle\right]=

\displaystyle p\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Sign}\left(\left\langle A,X\right\rangle-s\right)\left\langle A,Y\right\rangle|s\not=0\right]+(1-p)\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Sign}\left(\left\langle A,X\right\rangle\right)\left\langle A,Y\right\rangle\right].

This completes the proof.
∎

Now, we provide an overview of our proof technique for Theorem
4
. Let
\mathcal{G}_{Y}=\sup_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}}\mathcal{H}_{X,Y}
and
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}=\mathcal{G}_{Y}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{G}_{Y}]
be stochastic processes indexed by
Y
. According to Lemma
14
, it suffices to control
\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}}\mathcal{G}_{Y}
. We consider the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}}\mathcal{G}_{Y}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}+\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}}\mathbb{E}[{\mathcal{G}_{Y}}]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}+\frac{\varepsilon}{\zeta}\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}+\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{G}_{Y}]+\frac{\varepsilon}{\zeta}\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{G}_{Y}],

(68)

where the second inequality follows from
\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}\subset\mathbb{S}_{k}+\frac{\varepsilon}{\zeta}\mathbb{S}
, and the fact that both
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}
and
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{G}_{Y}]
are linear functions of
Y
. We control the individual terms in the above decomposition separately.
To provide an upper bound for
\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}
and
\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}
, we rely on the following key lemma.

Lemma 16
.

The stochastic processes
\{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\}_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}
and
\{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\}_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}
are
\mathcal{O}\left({1}/{m}\right)
-sub-Gaussian processes.

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{S}_{k}\subset\mathbb{S}
, it suffices to show that
\{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\}_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}
is
\mathcal{O}\left({1}/{m}\right)
-sub-Gaussian. According to Definition
7
, the stochastic process
\left\{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\right\}_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}
is sub-Gaussian if for any arbitrary
Y,Y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}
,
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}-\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y^{\prime}}
is
\mathcal{O}\left(\left\lVert Y-Y^{\prime}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}/m\right)
-sub-Gaussian. Note that
\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}-\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y^{\prime}}
is sub-Gaussian if and only if
\mathcal{G}_{Y}-\mathcal{G}_{Y^{\prime}}
is sub-Gaussian with the same parameter.
The latter will be proven by checking the moment bound condition in Definition
6
. For arbitrary
Y,Y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}
, denote
\Delta Y=Y-Y^{\prime}
. Then, for any
p\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathcal{G}_{Y}-\mathcal{G}_{Y^{\prime}}|^{2p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sup_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathrm{Sign}\left(\left\langle A_{i},X\right\rangle-s_{i}\right)\left\langle A_{i},\Delta Y\right\rangle-\varphi(X)\left\langle\frac{X}{\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}},\Delta Y\right\rangle\right|^{2p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\!\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sup_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}}\!\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathrm{Sign}\left(\left\langle A_{i},X\right\rangle\!-\!s_{i}\right)\left\langle A_{i},\Delta Y\right\rangle\right|\!+\!\!\!\sup_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}}\!\left|\varphi(X)\left\langle\frac{X}{\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}},\Delta Y\right\rangle\right|\right)^{2p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}|\left\langle A_{i},\Delta Y\right\rangle|+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\left\lVert\Delta Y\right\rVert_{F}\right)^{2p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2p}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}|\left\langle A_{i},\Delta Y\right\rangle|\right)^{2p}\right]+\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{p}\left\lVert\Delta Y\right\rVert_{F}^{2p}\right),

where in the third inequality, we used
\varphi(X)\leq\sqrt{2/\pi}
, which holds for every
X\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
. According to
[
20
, Appendix A.2]
, the random variable
({1}/{m})\sum_{i=1}^{m}|\left\langle A_{i},\Delta Y\right\rangle|
is
\mathcal{O}\left({\left\lVert\Delta Y\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}/{m}\right)
-sub-Gaussian with mean
\sqrt{{2}/{\pi}}\left\lVert\Delta Y\right\rVert_{F}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left|\left\langle A_{i},\Delta Y\right\rangle\right|\right)^{2p}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left|\left\langle A_{i},\Delta Y\right\rangle\right|-\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\|\Delta Y\|_{F}+\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\|\Delta Y\|_{F}\right)^{2p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2p}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left|\left\langle A_{i},\Delta Y\right\rangle\right|-\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\|\Delta Y\|_{F}\right)^{2p}\right]+\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{p}\|\Delta Y\|_{F}^{2p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2p}\left((2p-1)!!\frac{1}{m^{p}}\|\Delta Y\|_{F}^{2p}+\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{p}\|\Delta Y\|_{F}^{2p}\right).

Combining the above inequalities leads to

\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathcal{G}_{Y}-\mathcal{G}_{Y^{\prime}}|^{2p}\right]^{1/2p}\leq 4\left((2p-1)!!\frac{1}{m^{p}}\|\Delta Y\|_{F}^{2p}+\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{p}\|\Delta Y\|_{F}^{2p}\right)^{1/2p}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{p}{m}}\left\lVert\Delta Y\right\rVert_{F},

given that
p>m
. Therefore,
\{\mathcal{G}_{Y}\}_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}
is a
\mathcal{O}\left({1}/{m}\right)
-sub-Gaussian process, which implies that
\{\mathcal{G}_{Y}\}_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}
is also a
\mathcal{O}\left({1}/{m}\right)
-sub-Gaussian process.
∎

Given that both
\{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\}_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}
and
\{\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\}_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}
are sub-Gaussian processes, we can readily obtain sharp concentration bounds on their suprema.

Lemma 17
.

The following statements hold:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\right]\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{dk}{m}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\right]+\gamma\right)\lesssim e^{-cm\gamma^{2}};

(69)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\right]\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{d^{2}}{m}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\right]+\gamma\right)\lesssim e^{-cm\gamma^{2}}.

(70)

Proof.

The proof follows directly from Theorem
9
. The details are omitted for brevity.
∎

Equipped with the above lemma, we provide a concentration bound on
\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}
.

Lemma 18
.

Assume that
m\gtrsim\sqrt{dk/\gamma^{2}}
and
\varepsilon/\zeta\lesssim\sqrt{k/d}
. Then, the following inequality holds with probability of at least
1-Ce^{-cm\gamma^{2}}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\leq\left(3+\sqrt{\frac{k}{d}}\right)\gamma.

Proof.

Based on (
69
), we have, with probability of at least
1-Ce^{-cm\gamma^{2}}

\displaystyle\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\right]+\gamma\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{dk}{m}}+\gamma\leq 2\gamma,

where the last inequality follows from the assumption
m\gtrsim\sqrt{dk/\gamma^{2}}
. Similarly, based on (
70
), the following inequalities hold with a probability of at least
1-Ce^{-cm\gamma^{2}}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{\zeta}\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\zeta}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\right]+\gamma\right)\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\varepsilon^{2}d^{2}}{\zeta^{2}m}}+\frac{\varepsilon\gamma}{\zeta}\leq\left(1+\sqrt{\frac{k}{d}}\right)\gamma,

where the last inequality follows from
m\gtrsim\sqrt{dk/\gamma^{2}}
and
\varepsilon/\zeta\lesssim\sqrt{k/d}
. Finally, a simple union bound implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\leq\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}+\frac{\varepsilon}{\zeta}\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}\bar{\mathcal{G}}_{Y}\leq\left(3+\sqrt{\frac{k}{d}}\right)\gamma,

with probability of at least
1-Ce^{-cm\gamma^{2}}
, thereby completing the proof.
∎

Recalling (
B.2
), it remains to control the terms
\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{G}_{Y}]
and
\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{G}_{Y}]
.

Lemma 19
.

The following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{G}_{Y}]\leq\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{G}_{Y}]\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{dk}{m}\log^{2}(m)\log\left(\frac{R}{\zeta}\right)}.

(71)

Due to its length, we defer the proof of Lemma
19
to Appendix
F.1
. Equipped with Lemmas
18
and
19
, we are ready to present the proof of Theorem
4
.

Proof of Theorem
4
.
The inequality (
B.2
) combined with Lemmas
18
and
19
implies that the following inequality holds with probability of at least
1-Ce^{-cm\gamma^{2}}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}}\mathcal{G}_{Y}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{dk}{m}\log^{2}(m)\log\left(\frac{R}{\zeta}\right)}+\gamma.

On the other hand, one can write

\inf_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}}\varphi(X)=\inf_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}}\left\{\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\left(1-p+p\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-s^{2}/(2\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}^{2})}\right]\right)\right\}\geq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}(1-p).

(72)

Therefore, upon choosing

\displaystyle m\gtrsim\frac{dk\log^{2}(m)\log\left(R/\zeta\right)}{(1-p)^{2}\delta^{2}},\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma\lesssim(1-p)\delta,

we have

\displaystyle\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}}\mathcal{G}_{Y}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}\\
Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{H}_{X,Y}\leq\inf_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}}\varphi(X)\delta

with probability of at least
1-Ce^{-cm(1-p)^{2}\delta^{2}}
. This completes the proof of Theorem
4
.
\hfill\square

B.3
Proof of Theorem
5

Recall that, with outlier noise model, the scaling function takes the form
\varphi(X)=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\left(1-p+p\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-s^{2}/(2\|X\|_{F}^{2})}\right]\right)
. Setting
p=1
, and
s_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\nu_{g}^{2})
immediately implies
\varphi(X)=\mathbb{E}_{s\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\nu_{g}^{2})}\left[e^{-s^{2}/(2\|X\|_{F}^{2})}\right]=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}}{\sqrt{\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\nu_{g}^{2}}}
. On the other hand, using the same method in the proof of Theorem
4
, we can show that the following inequality holds with probability at least
1-Ce^{-cm\gamma^{2}}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}}\mathcal{G}_{Y}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{dk}{m}\log^{2}(m)\log\left(\frac{R}{\zeta}\right)}+\gamma,

where
\mathcal{G}_{Y}
is defined in the proof of Theorem
4
. It remains to bound
\inf_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}}\varphi(X)
.
To this goal, note that
\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}\geq\zeta,\forall X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}
. Hence, we have the following lower bound for the scaling function

\inf_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}}\varphi(X)=\inf_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}}{\sqrt{\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\nu_{g}^{2}}}\geq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{\zeta}{\sqrt{\zeta^{2}+\nu_{g}^{2}}}\gtrsim\frac{\zeta}{\nu_{g}},

(73)

provided that
\zeta\lesssim\nu_{g}
.
Therefore, upon choosing
m\gtrsim\frac{\nu_{g}^{2}dk\log^{2}(m)\log\left(R/\zeta\right)}{\zeta^{2}\delta^{2}}
and
\gamma\lesssim\frac{\zeta}{\nu_{g}}\delta
, we have

\sup_{Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}}\mathcal{G}_{Y}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}\\
Y\in\mathbb{S}_{k,\varepsilon/\zeta}\end{subarray}}\mathcal{H}_{X,Y}\leq\inf_{X\in\mathcal{S}_{k,\varepsilon}}\varphi(X)\delta,

(74)

with probability at least
1-C_{1}e^{-C_{2}m\zeta^{2}\delta^{2}/\nu_{g}^{2}}
, which completes the proof.

\hfill\square

B.4
Proof of Lemma
1

Lemma
20
is a direct consequence of the following more general result:

Lemma 20
.

Suppose that the measurements are noiseless, and satisfy
(k,\delta,\varepsilon,\mathcal{S})
-Sign-RIP for any
\varepsilon\geq 0
,
\mathcal{S}
, and scaling function
\varphi(\cdot)
. Then, for any
\varepsilon
-approximate rank-
k
matrix
X\in\mathcal{S}
, we have

\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left|\left\langle A_{i},X\right\rangle\right|-\varphi(X)\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}\right|\leq\delta\varphi(X)\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}.

Proof.

According to the definition of Sign-RIP, for every
\varepsilon
-approximate rank-
k
matrices
X,Y\in\mathcal{S}
, and every
Q\in\mathcal{Q}(X)
, we have
\left|\left\langle Q-\varphi(X)\frac{X}{\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}},\frac{Y}{\|Y\|_{F}}\right\rangle\right|\leq\varphi(X)\delta
. In particular, upon choosing
Y=X
, we have

\left|\left\langle Q-\varphi(X)\frac{X}{\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}},X\right\rangle\right|=\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left|\left\langle A_{i},X\right\rangle\right|-\varphi(X)\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}\right|\leq\varphi(X)\delta\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{F}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Evidently, the result of Lemma
20
can be readily recovered from the above lemma after substituting
X
with
\Delta_{t}
.
Moreover, it is worth noting that the above lemma recovers the so-called
\ell_{1}/\ell_{2}
-RIP introduced in
[
20
]
with
\varepsilon=0
and
\varphi(X)=\sqrt{2/\pi}
.

B.5
Proof of Lemma
2

Due to Sign-RIP, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert Q_{t}\right\rVert\leq\varphi(\Delta_{t})\left(\frac{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}}+\delta\right)

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\lVert Q_{t}\right\rVert}\geq\frac{1}{\varphi(\Delta_{t})}\frac{1}{\delta+\frac{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}}}

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left\lVert Q_{t}\right\rVert}\geq\frac{1}{\varphi(\Delta_{t})}\frac{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\left(1-\delta\frac{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\right)

(75)

By our assumption,
\Delta_{t}
is
\varepsilon
-approximate rank-
k
. This implies that, there exists a rank-
k
matrix
X^{\prime}
such that
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}-X^{\prime}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\varepsilon
. This in turn implies that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\Delta_{t}-X^{\prime}\right\rVert_{F}+\left\lVert X^{\prime}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\varepsilon+\left\lVert X^{\prime}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\varepsilon+\sqrt{k}\left\lVert X^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq(1+\sqrt{k})\varepsilon+\sqrt{k}\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\geq\left\lVert X^{\prime}\right\rVert-\left\lVert\Delta_{t}-X^{\prime}\right\rVert\geq\left\lVert X^{\prime}\right\rVert-\varepsilon\geq\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert-2\varepsilon\geq\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert/2

where in the last inequality we used the assumption
\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert\geq 4\varepsilon
. Combining the above inequalities implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}\leq\frac{2(1+\sqrt{k})\varepsilon}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}+2\sqrt{k}\leq\frac{1+5\sqrt{k}}{2}.

Combining the above inequality with (
75
) leads to

\displaystyle\eta_{t}=\frac{\eta\rho^{t}}{\left\lVert Q_{t}\right\rVert}\geq\left(1-\left(\frac{1+5\sqrt{k}}{2}\right)\delta\right)\frac{\eta\rho^{t}}{\varphi(\Delta_{t})}\frac{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert_{F}}{\left\lVert\Delta_{t}\right\rVert}.

The upper bound can be derived in a similar fashion.
\hfill\square

Appendix C
Proofs of the Expected Loss

C.1
Proof of Proposition
2

According to the update rule
U_{t+1}=U_{t}-\eta\left(U_{t}U_{t}^{\top}-X^{\star}\right)U_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle S_{t+1}

\displaystyle=V^{\top}U_{t+1}=S_{t}-\eta\left(\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)S_{t}+S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}\right).

Define an auxiliary matrix
M
as

M:=\left(I-\Xi\right)\left(S_{t}-\eta\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)S_{t}\right)\left(S_{t}^{\top}-\eta S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)\right)\left(I-\Xi^{\top}\right),

(76)

where
\Xi=\eta S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\left(I-\eta\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)\right)^{-1}
. Note that, due to our assumptions and the choice of
\eta
, we have
S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\succ 0
and
\eta\left\lVert S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right\rVert<1
. Therefore, both matrices
S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}
and
I-\eta\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)
are invertible and the matrix
\Xi
is well-defined. Our goal is to first show that
\lambda_{\min}(S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top})\geq\lambda_{\min}(M)
, and then derive a lower bound for
\lambda_{\min}(M)
. Based on the definition of
\Xi
, it is easy to verify that

\Xi\left(S_{t}-\eta\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)S_{t}\right)=S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}\mathsf{P}_{S_{t}}.

Combining this with (
76
) reveals that

\displaystyle S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}-M

\displaystyle=\eta^{2}S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}\mathsf{P}_{S_{t}}^{\perp}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\succeq 0.

Therefore, instead of obtaining a lower bound for
\lambda_{\min}(S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top})
, it suffices to obtain a lower bound for
\lambda_{\min}(M)
.
One can write

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Xi\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\eta\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\right\rVert\left\lVert\left(I-\eta\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)\right)^{-1}\right\rVert

(77)

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\eta\left\lVert\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1/2}S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1/2}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\eta\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\left\lVert\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1/2}S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1/2}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\eta\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert.

\displaystyle<1,

where the last inequality is due to the assumption
\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq\sigma_{1}
and the choice of
\eta
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{\min}\left(M\right)

(78)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\left\lVert\Xi\right\rVert\right)^{2}\lambda_{\min}\left(\left(S_{t}-\eta\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)S_{t}\right)\left(S_{t}^{\top}-\eta S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)\right)\right).

Now it suffices to bound
\lambda_{\min}\left(\left(S_{t}-\eta\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)S_{t}\right)\left(S_{t}^{\top}-\eta S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)\right)\right)
. First note that

S_{t}-\eta\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)S_{t}=\left(I+\eta\Sigma\left(I-\eta S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\right)\left(S_{t}-\eta S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}S_{t}\right).

Based on the above equality, one can write

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(\left(S_{t}-\eta\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)S_{t}\right)\left(S_{t}^{\top}-\eta S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)\right)\right)

(79)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\min}\left(\left(I+\eta\Sigma\left(I-\eta S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\right)\left(S_{t}-\eta S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}S_{t}\right)\left(S_{t}^{\top}-\eta S_{t}^{\top}S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\left(I+\eta\Sigma\left(I-\eta S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{\min}\left(\left(I+\eta\Sigma\left(I-\eta S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\right)\right)^{2}\lambda_{\min}\left(\left(S_{t}-\eta S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}S_{t}\right)\left(S_{t}^{\top}-\eta S_{t}^{\top}S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\geq}}\left(1+\eta\sigma_{r}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\right)^{-1}\right)^{2}\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-2\eta\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{2}+\eta^{2}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{3}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\left(1+\eta\sigma_{r}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\right)^{-1}\right)^{2}\left(\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)-2\eta\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{2}+\eta^{2}\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{3}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1+\eta\sigma_{r}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\right)^{-1}\right)^{2}\left(\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)-2\eta\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1+\eta\sigma_{r}\right)^{2}\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)-2\eta\left(1+\eta\sigma_{r}\right)\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{2},

where in (a), we used the fact that

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}\left(I+\eta\Sigma\left(I-\eta S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=1+\lambda_{\min}\left(\eta\Sigma\left(I-\eta S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=1+\eta\lambda_{\min}\left(\Sigma^{1/2}\left(I-\eta S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\Sigma^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1+\eta\sigma_{r}\lambda_{\min}\left(\left(I-\eta S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=1+\eta\sigma_{r}\left(1-\eta\lambda_{\min}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\right)^{-1},

and in (b) we used the fact that the matrices
S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}
,
(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top})^{2}
, and
(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top})^{3}
share the same eigenvectors. Combining (
79
) with (
77
) and (
78
) completes the proof.

\hfill\square

C.2
Proof of Proposition
3

Before delving into the details, we provide the update rule for
S_{t}
and
E_{t}
, which will be used frequently throughout our proof. Applying the signal-residual decomposition to
U_{t+1}=U_{t}-\eta\left(U_{t}U_{t}^{\top}-X^{\star}\right)U_{t}
leads to

S_{t+1}=V^{\top}U_{t+1}=S_{t}-\eta\left(\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)S_{t}+S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}\right),

(80)

E_{t+1}=V_{\perp}^{\top}U_{t+1}=E_{t}-\eta E_{t}\left(S_{t}^{\top}S_{t}+E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}\right).

(81)

Bounding
\left\lVert\Sigma-S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}\right\rVert
:

The update rule for
S_{t+1}
leads to

\displaystyle\Sigma\!-\!S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\!\underbrace{\Sigma\!-\!S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\!+\!\eta S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\!-\!\Sigma\right)\!+\!\eta\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\!-\!\Sigma\right)S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\!-\!\eta^{2}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\!-\!\Sigma\right)S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\!-\!\Sigma\right)}_{(A)}

(82)

\displaystyle\underbrace{+2\eta S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\eta^{2}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-{\eta^{2}S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}S^{\top}_{t}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)}}_{(B)}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\eta^{2}S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}}_{(C)}.

First, we provide an upper bound for (B). Recall that
\eta=\mathcal{O}\left({1}/{\sigma_{1}}\right)
. Moreover, we have
\left\lVert S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right\rVert\leq 2.01\sigma_{1}
due to the assumption
\left\lVert S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq 1.01\sigma_{1}
. Therefore, one can write

\left\lVert(B)\right\rVert\leq 2\eta\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert+2\eta^{2}\left\lVert S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right\rVert\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}E_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq 4\eta\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}.

Similarly, due to our assumption on
\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert
and
\eta
, we have
\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\leq 1/\eta
, which in turn implies

\left\lVert(C)\right\rVert\leq\eta^{2}\left\lVert E_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\eta\left\lVert S_{t}E_{t}^{\top}\right\rVert^{2}.

Finally, we provide an upper bound for (A). First, one can verify that

\displaystyle(A)=\underbrace{\left(\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\left(0.5I-\eta S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)}_{(A_{1})}+\underbrace{\left(0.5I-\eta S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}+\eta^{2}\left(S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma\right)S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)\left(\Sigma-S_{t}S_{t}^{\top}\right)}_{(A_{2})}.

For the first term, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert(A_{1})\right\rVert
